| 2026-10-20 | **S01 · PR 22 follow-up (photos showed only the colour box):** a real bug in the storage mock, not in sharp: each storage adapter's MOCK mode built a new, empty in-memory store on every connection lookup, and the media service writes a file through "where files go now" but reads it back through "the provider it was written to" — two lookups, two stores — so the size job never found the original and no size was made. Only setups without `EXTAS_MEDIA_DIR` (a laptop, CI, a preview) are hit; the VM sets `EXTAS_MEDIA_DIR=/media` (local disk, shared by the replicas). Fix: one in-memory store per provider for the whole process (`sharedMemoryStorage`, kept on globalThis), a log line once when the app runs on it; tests: the adapter lookup paths (adapters) and upload → 15 sizes → served by a second runtime on PostgreSQL (media). |

| 2026-10-31 | **PR 4 follow-up · stack repair after the store move (GitHub #66; Progress unchanged).** Deploy of 42b8f00 rolled back again: inside the running extas-postgres `tr` and pg_isready were missing. Cause: the old-copy removal ran through a shared bind view of / that the new LV had reached (lsblk showed the LV at /tmp/tmp.*/var/lib/containerd), so it deleted files in the live store. Now the view is private and checked to be /'s own file system; `osdisk_repair_images` takes down stale views, recreates broken or stopped stack containers, re-pulls broken pulled images (`ctr images rm --sync`), removes broken built-here images for `--build`. If containerd's meta.db was also deleted, a containerd restart loses the image list: re-pull and redeploy rebuild it (data is in host folders). |

The live run of 1 Nov 13:03Z: gate-orders stopped in scenario 13 (tablet, the Add press got no answer) and its report said nothing about the owner's cash-on-delivery switch; gate-c360 failed its search and wishlist steps. **Cash on delivery: the 13:03Z run did not leave it on** — the shell's safety net cleaned up after the crash and closed the grant (`mockPay closed 1, codRestored 1`, the exact earlier value, version-checked); only the report did not say so. Each scenario has its own time budget (`scenarioBudgetMs`, 10–30 min) and try/catch; a timeout or crash is its FAIL row with the error and `scenario-<n>.jpg`; the report is written after every scenario. Root causes: **scenario 13** — the bag's `waitForResponse` was created, the click hung past it and the rejected wait had no handler, so Node stopped the whole run (not reproduced locally; the press now has its own timeout, the wait is always handled, `pressBlocker` names what took the tap; each scenario's pieces stocked up first); **c360 search and wishlist** — the GATE TEST products had no category and the shop shows only products in a shown category (search, lists and the wishlist page filter on it); the gate now makes its own shown category, and the search step waits up to `waits.searchSec` (90 s) for the word list; Bengali and typo search are built (PR 72), no SKIP; no app bug; the wishlist step waits for the save's answer and taps again only if it was not saved. Tests: `apps/web/src/lib/shop/gate-cod.db.test.ts` (8, PostgreSQL), tools 65, infra 510 PASS. New `lib/legacy-browser.test.ts` (9 cases, pure). `tools/admin-session-e2e.mjs` gained step 0 (pre-seeds a legacy cookie, 200 KB of localStorage, a Cache Storage entry and a service-worker registration before opening `/admin/login` — proving sign-in still works with all of it present) and step 7 (opens `/reset` at the end and checks every one of those is gone). One admin-guide line added (`docs/admin-guide/login.md`). Verified locally: `apps/web` eslint and typecheck clean on every touched file, `vitest run src/lib/legacy-browser.test.ts src/lib/seo/robots.test.ts` green, `tools/docs/precommit-docs.sh` clean; `admin-session-e2e.mjs`'s new steps verified by CI's own `sign-in end to end` job (needs a running build + Playwright, not available in this sandbox). `docs/MASTER-REGISTER.md` (PR 12's row + the Updated-lines summary) updated in the same commit; no register count changes (an Updated line, not a new PR). Registered as an Updated clause on PR 12 (CN-045) in `docs/MASTER-REGISTER.md`. | 2026-11-12 | **CN-045 job 1 — PR #182's own CI kept failing after the fix above (commit `b3c68dbb`), with the exact same "sign-in end to end" symptom.** First diagnosis (defensive middleware fix answering `/reset`'s `Clear-Site-Data` from `proxy.ts` as well as the route) was wrong and did not help — confirmed by the build log that `/reset` is already `ƒ` (dynamic) in Next's own route table, ruling out static caching. **Real root cause, found by running the sandbox's own Playwright/Chromium against a local `next dev` server:** `tools/smoke.mjs`'s `resetPageProblems` read the header with Playwright's `response.headers()` convenience object — which silently drops `Clear-Site-Data` in real Chromium (the browser receives and acts on it; the dictionary just omits it) — while `response.headerValue('clear-site-data')` and the raw `headersArray()` both see it correctly. The route (`apps/web/src/app/reset/route.ts`) was never broken. **Fix:** reverted `b3c68dbb` (`git revert --no-edit`, then squashed into one compliant commit per `check-commits.mjs`) and changed `resetPageProblems` to read the header with `headerValue()` instead of `headers()`; added two `smoke.test.mjs` cases pinning this exact Playwright quirk (25/25 tests green). Pushed as commit `0d9cf3a6`; PR #182 CI re-running. **Next:** confirm PR #182 green, merge, redeploy, verify `/reset` + `/admin/login` on the live main host, then lift the freeze and merge the backlog (`#149` confirmed clean, `#124`, `#178`, `#181`, plus `#179`/`#184`/`#185` once their own lanes finish), then tell the owner in Bengali. |

Honest scope, same instinct as PR 307/308 (S15): the real dispatcher that would pick products/templates and fire a render+post on schedule needs both the real compositor (PR 439's own gap) and a `packages/jobs` cron slot not wired for this stage, so it stays unbuilt rather than faked. `publish.ts` gains what PR 442 *does* enforce for real: the approval queue (`decideSocialPost`, `studio.approve` — approve moves a `PENDING_APPROVAL` post to `SCHEDULED`/`PUBLISHING` exactly where it would have landed had the account already run free; reject is final); reschedule (`rescheduleSocialPost`, `studio.generate`) moves a not-yet-sent post to a new time or clears it; the calendar (`studioCalendar`, `studio.read`) groups every post with a `scheduledAt`/`publishedAt` by the store's own calendar day; `publishDuePosts` (`studio.publish`, idempotent — an `updateMany` claim guard makes a second run of the same due post a no-op, the same "safe to run twice" shape `@extas/customers`'s `runEmailSends` already uses) is the explicit "send what's due" step a person (or later a worker) presses; every path that can actually publish now also checks the account's own `dailyLimit` (PR 437) and, for Instagram, the platform's own ~50/day ceiling (`PLATFORM_DAILY_CAP`, a platform fact not a business number, CN-018). A `PENDING_APPROVAL` post gets its own `AiApprovalRequest` row (the shared S15 AI 360 table, `growth-agent.ts`'s own `GROWTH_KIND` the closest precedent) so a future central queue page can list it. Admin page gains approve/reject and reschedule controls on each post, a content-calendar section (next two weeks, grouped by day), an auto-schedule-rules section (add/switch/archive, weekday checkboxes, a "next active day" preview) and a "run due posts now" button; `postPermission` routing extended with `studio.approve`/`studio.publish`. Tests: 5 pure (`schedule.test.ts`) + 3 real-PostgreSQL (`schedule.db.test.ts`) for the rule CRUD; 6 new real-PostgreSQL cases in `publish.db.test.ts` (approval-request creation, approve/reject, scheduled-then-approved, free-account calendar + reschedule, daily-limit refusal on both the free and approval paths, `publishDuePosts` sending exactly what's due and staying a no-op on a second run); 2 new web `.db.test.ts` cases (rule add/switch/archive through the page's own API; hold → approve → reschedule → calendar → `schedule.runDue` end to end, with permission denials at each step). Verified locally: `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=6144` raised Node heap for `@extas/studio`/`@extas/web` typecheck (both clean) — the shared VM's concurrent lanes were hitting the default heap limit, and `/tmp/node-compile-cache` (943 MB, regenerable) was cleared first since the container's writable disk was at 98%; eslint clean on every touched file; hardcode-guard 0/3168 files; `admin-guide-check` clean; `pnpm docs:stages` reports "nothing changed" (the stage pages were already regenerated); the package's own pure tests green (10/10); the real-PostgreSQL `.db.test.ts` cases could not be run directly in this sandbox (no reachable `TEST_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL` credential here) and are left to CI's own `db` job, the same gate every prior S23 PR relied on for its PostgreSQL coverage. `docs/admin-guide/pages/marketing/studio.md`, `docs/stages/bn.json`, README and `docs/MASTER-REGISTER.md` (Build status row, Progress line, Open/closed line) updated in the same commit. Next: open the PR, watch CI green (including the `db` job's real run of this PR's new PostgreSQL tests), merge, then PR 443 (comment/DM ingestion from Facebook, Instagram and TikTok into the Support Inbox) — the last S23 row. |

`disk-tidy.sh` also protects the newest `DEPLOY_KEEP_GOOD_GENERATIONS` (2) good versions, not just one, as a safety margin; (3) `deploy-report.sh` now always says, in bold, which version (old or new, by commit) is the one live when a rollback itself fails — from a fresh post-rollback health check (`deploy.yml`'s new "Confirm what is live" step) when there is one, else from `deploy.sh`'s own guarantee that a failed rollback never changes what was live. 8 new `infra/tests/run.sh` checks simulate both failure modes (a crash with no result.json; the rollback target gone, with and without an older good version still present) and prove the right version is restored or, when truly none is left, that the report says so plainly. `infra/tests/run.sh` 530/530, `tools/smoke.test.mjs` 25/25, `node --check tools/smoke.mjs`, `shellcheck -S warning` clean on every touched script |
